## Randolf Pohl

↑ **Parent:** [Physicist](physicist.md)

A physicist whose collaborative [muonic atom](physics.md#muonic-atom) spectroscopy measured the [proton](physics.md#proton) finite-size contribution to the muonic-hydrogen [Lamb shift](atomic-physics.md#lamb-shift).[Author list and experiment, Nature 466, pp. 213–216 (2010)](physics-bibliography.md#the-size-of-the-proton-by-randolf-pohl-and-others)

### Work by Randolf Pohl

### H. Wagner

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)

Coauthor of the 1966 paper with [N. D. Mermin](#n-d-mermin) on the absence of magnetic order in low-dimensional isotropic [Heisenberg models](angular-momentum-operator.md#heisenberg-model). The primary publication identifies the author as [H. Wagner](#h-wagner). [https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.17.1133](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.17.1133)

#### Work by H. Wagner

↑ **Parent:** [H. Wagner](#h-wagner)

### N. D. Mermin

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)

Coauthor of the 1966 proof excluding finite-temperature magnetic order in finite-range one- and two-dimensional isotropic [Heisenberg models](angular-momentum-operator.md#heisenberg-model). The primary publication identifies the author as [N. D. Mermin](#n-d-mermin). [https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.17.1133](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.17.1133)

#### Work by N. D. Mermin

↑ **Parent:** [N. D. Mermin](#n-d-mermin)

### William Bertozzi

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)

An experimental nuclear physicist whose [electron](physics.md#electron) speed-and-energy measurement provides a direct test of relativistic kinematics. [Bertozzi's electron speed-energy experiment](special-relativity.md#bertozzi-s-electron-speed-energy-experiment) measures flight time and calorimetric [energy](energy.md) independently.

Official profile: [https://physics.mit.edu/faculty/william-bertozzi/](https://physics.mit.edu/faculty/william-bertozzi/)

### Charles Kittel

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)

His research with [Malvin Ruderman](#malvin-ruderman) helped explain [conduction-electron-mediated exchange](electromagnetism.md#conduction-electron-mediated-exchange). Berkeley collects recollections of his research and teaching: [https://physics.berkeley.edu/node/808.](https://physics.berkeley.edu/node/808.)

<a id="image-charles-kittel-from-the-university-of-california-berkeley"></a>
<img src="https://physics.berkeley.edu/sites/default/files/styles/openberkeley_image_full/public/general/kittelmemorium_2.jpg?itok=FmEUOT7v&amp;timestamp=1649695451" alt="" height="240">

**[Figure 3](#image-charles-kittel-from-the-university-of-california-berkeley). Charles Kittel, from the University of California, Berkeley**.

#### Work by Charles Kittel

↑ **Parent:** [Charles Kittel](#charles-kittel)

### Malvin Ruderman

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)

With [Charles Kittel](#charles-kittel), he calculated [conduction-electron-mediated exchange](electromagnetism.md#conduction-electron-mediated-exchange) between nuclear magnetic moments. His later research included [neutron stars](stellar-astrophysics.md#neutron-star). Columbia's account of his career also recalls his enthusiasm for order-of-magnitude teaching.[Columbia University, In Memoriam: Professor Mal Ruderman, 25 July 2024](https://fas.columbia.edu/news/memoriam-professor-mal-ruderman)

<a id="image-malvin-ruderman-from-columbia-university-s-memorial"></a>

**[Figure 4](#image-malvin-ruderman-from-columbia-university-s-memorial). Malvin Ruderman, from Columbia University's memorial**.

#### Work by Malvin Ruderman

↑ **Parent:** [Malvin Ruderman](#malvin-ruderman)

### Clarence Zener

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)

His work on [double exchange](electromagnetism.md#double-exchange-mechanism) connected carrier motion with [ferromagnetism](electromagnetism.md#ferromagnetism) in mixed-valence materials. Carnegie Mellon preserves his research papers: [https://findingaids.library.cmu.edu/repositories/2/resources/93.](https://findingaids.library.cmu.edu/repositories/2/resources/93.)

### James Prescott Joule

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Prescott_Joule)

James Prescott Joule was an English [physicist](physicist.md) whose private experiments connected [electricity](electromagnetism.md#electricity), [heat](heat.md) and mechanical [work](energy.md#work). His family ran a brewery in Salford; he developed an experimental laboratory while working in the family business.[Biographical account: brewing family, private laboratory and experimental interests](physics-bibliography.md#james-prescott-joule-by-science-museum-group) The Royal Society's archive identifies his occupation as [physicist](physicist.md) and records his life as 1818–1889.[Person record NA7041: occupation and life dates](physics-bibliography.md#james-prescott-joule-by-royal-society)

<a id="image-portrait-of-james-prescott-joule-1882"></a>
<img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/6a/James_Prescott_Joule_by_John_Collier%2C_1882.jpg" alt="" width="250">

**[Figure 10](#image-portrait-of-james-prescott-joule-1882). Portrait of James Prescott Joule, 1882**. [Source](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:James\_Prescott\_Joule\_by\_John\_Collier,\_1882.jpg).

His electrical heating experiments placed metal coils in water and measured the [temperature](temperature.md) change. Later, he used the brewery cellar to reduce ambient [temperature](temperature.md) fluctuations during friction experiments and took particular care over [thermometer calibration](temperature.md#thermometer-calibration).[Electrical heating experiments; brewery-cellar friction measurements and precision thermometers](physics-bibliography.md#james-prescott-joule-by-science-museum-group) These practical choices address a central difficulty of [calorimetry](calorimetry.md): a small [temperature](temperature.md) rise can reflect both the intended input of [energy](energy.md) and unwanted [heat transfer](heat.md#heat-transfer) to the surroundings. A quiet baseline and a known [heat capacity](heat.md#heat-capacity) are part of the measurement, not decoration around the formula.

The contrast between [Joule heating](electric-charge.md#joule-heating) and [Joule's paddle-wheel experiment](internal-energy.md#joule-s-paddle-wheel-experiment) makes the connection especially clear. One supplies electrical [work](energy.md#work); the other supplies mechanical [work](energy.md#work). In each case, [calorimetry](calorimetry.md) tracks the resulting change of [internal energy](internal-energy.md). Comparing quantitatively controlled inputs with thermal changes gives an experimental test of [conservation of energy](mathematical-physics.md#conservation-of-energy), rather than merely showing that stirring or an [electric current](electric-charge.md#electric-current) can warm water. The paddle-wheel account describes the descending weights, vanes, thermal controls and corrections needed for that comparison.

[Joule's free-expansion experiment](thermodynamics.md#joule-s-free-expansion-experiment) asks a different question: how does a gas's [temperature](temperature.md) change during expansion without external [work](energy.md#work)? The [Joule-Thomson effect](enthalpy.md#joule-thomson-effect) instead concerns flow through a restriction and a balance involving [enthalpy](enthalpy.md). Keeping the two experiments distinct prevents a shared historical name from hiding different system boundaries and different conserved quantities.

#### Work by James Prescott Joule

↑ **Parent:** [James Prescott Joule](#james-prescott-joule)

### Hendrik Antoon Lorentz

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)

A [physicist](physicist.md).[Biographical identity and professional specialization](https://www.nobelprize.org/prizes/physics/1902/lorentz/biographical/)

The [Lorentz force](relativistic-electromagnetism.md#lorentz-force) connects charged-particle motion to the [electric field](electric-field.md) and [magnetic field](magnetic-field.md). His theoretical account of the [Zeeman effect](atomic-physics.md#zeeman-splitting) connects that motion to changes in emitted [light](optics.md#light).

### Joseph John Thomson

A [physicist](physicist.md).[Biographical identity and professional specialization](https://www.nobelprize.org/prizes/physics/1906/thomson/biographical/)

**[Figure 61](#image-portrait-of-joseph-john-thomson). Portrait of Joseph John Thomson**. [Source](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:JJ_Thomson.jpg).

The [Thomson charge-to-mass experiment](relativistic-electromagnetism.md#thomson-charge-to-mass-experiment) used electric and magnetic deflections of [cathode rays](physics.md#cathode-ray) to investigate their charged constituents. It determines a [charge-to-mass ratio](relativistic-electromagnetism.md#charge-to-mass-ratio); separating [electric charge](electric-charge.md) from [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass) requires additional information.

#### Work by Joseph John Thomson

The [Stern-Gerlach experiment](angular-momentum-operator.md#stern-gerlach-experiment), with [Otto Stern](#otto-stern), sent a neutral [silver](chemical-substance.md#silver) [atomic beam](physics.md#atomic-beam) through a nonuniform [magnetic field](magnetic-field.md) and recorded its deposition pattern.

### Henri Becquerel

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henri_Becquerel)

A [physicist](physicist.md) whose [uranium](chemical-substance.md#uranium) experiments helped establish [radioactivity](physics.md#radioactive-decay). His 1896 [photographic plate](optical-instrument.md#photographic-plate) tests separated exposure to the material from immediate illumination; the cautious interpretation in his report is part of the story of [Becquerel's uranium-radiation experiment](physics.md#becquerel-s-uranium-radiation-experiment). He held the chair of applied [physics](physics.md) at the Paris Museum of Natural History.[Nobel Lectures, Physics 1901–1921; Biographical identity and professional specialization](physics-bibliography.md#nobel-lectures-physics-1901-1921/henri-becquerel-biographical)

### Marie Curie

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marie_Curie)

A [physicist](physicist.md) and [chemist](chemistry.md#chemist) whose investigation of [radioactivity](physics.md#radioactive-decay) joined sensitive measurement to chemical separation. She moved from Warsaw to Paris to study, later becoming a professor of [physics](physics.md). The [Curie ionization-current measurement](physics.md#curie-ionization-current-measurement) shows the experimental craft behind the work: balancing a tiny [electric current](electric-charge.md#electric-current) against a calibrated [electric charge](electric-charge.md). She shared the 1903 [Nobel Prize in Physics](nobel-prize-in-physics.md) and received the 1911 prize in [chemistry](chemistry.md).[Nobel Lectures, Physics 1901–1921; Biographical identity and professional specialization](physics-bibliography.md#nobel-lectures-physics-1901-1921/marie-curie-biographical)

<a id="image-portrait-of-marie-curie"></a>
<img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/c8/Marie_Curie_c._1920s.jpg" alt="" width="240">

**[Figure 64](#image-portrait-of-marie-curie). Portrait of Marie Curie**. [Source](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Marie_Curie_c._1920s.jpg).

#### Work by Marie Curie

↑ **Parent:** [Marie Curie](#marie-curie)

### Pierre Curie

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_Curie)

A [physicist](physicist.md) who investigated [magnetism](electromagnetism.md#magnetism), [piezoelectricity](quantum-mechanics.md#piezoelectricity) and [radioactivity](physics.md#radioactive-decay). He built delicate balances, [electrometers](electric-charge.md#electrometer) and [piezoelectric crystals](quantum-mechanics.md#piezoelectric-crystal); this work on instruments helped make weak electrical effects measurable. With [Marie Curie](#marie-curie), he investigated radioactive substances under difficult laboratory conditions. The [Curie ionization-current measurement](physics.md#curie-ionization-current-measurement) illustrates how such instrumentation can turn a small signal into a quantitative comparison.[Nobel Lectures, Physics 1901–1921; Biographical identity and professional specialization](physics-bibliography.md#nobel-lectures-physics-1901-1921/pierre-curie-biographical)

<a id="image-portrait-of-pierre-curie"></a>

**[Figure 65](#image-portrait-of-pierre-curie). Portrait of Pierre Curie**. [Source](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Pierre_Curie_by_Dujardin_c1906.jpg).

#### Work by Pierre Curie

↑ **Parent:** [Pierre Curie](#pierre-curie)

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)

**[Figure 66](#image-portrait-of-wilhelm-conrad-rontgen). Portrait of Wilhelm Conrad Röntgen**.

The [Röntgen X-ray discovery experiment](electromagnetic-radiation.md#rontgen-x-ray-discovery-experiment) connected a covered discharge tube, a fluorescent screen and photographic exposures. The [X-ray](electromagnetic-radiation.md#x-ray) article follows those tests into modern source and attenuation models.

### Pieter Zeeman

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)

A [physicist](physicist.md).[Biographical identity and professional specialization](https://www.nobelprize.org/nomination/archive/show.php?id=7221)

<a id="image-portrait-of-pieter-zeeman"></a>
<img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/7/71/Pieter_Zeeman_1902.jpg" alt="" width="240">

**[Figure 67](#image-portrait-of-pieter-zeeman). Portrait of Pieter Zeeman**. [Source](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Pieter_Zeeman_1902.jpg).

The [Zeeman effect](atomic-physics.md#zeeman-splitting) connects a laboratory [magnetic field](magnetic-field.md) to the frequencies and [wave polarization](wave.md#polarization-waves) of [light](optics.md#light) emitted by [chemical atoms](physics.md#atom-physics). Its experimental history makes the viewing direction and spectral resolving power essential to the inference.

#### Work by Pieter Zeeman

↑ **Parent:** [Pieter Zeeman](#pieter-zeeman)

The [early two-stage electron microscope experiment](optics.md#early-two-stage-electron-microscope-experiment) investigates how magnetic electron lenses can form a magnified image. The [electron microscope](optics.md#electron-microscope) article distinguishes [magnification](optics.md#magnification) from [electron microscope resolution](optics.md#electron-microscope-resolution).

### Albert Abraham Michelson

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)

A [physicist](physicist.md).[Independent biographical identity and professional specialization](https://www.nasonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/michelson-a-a.pdf)

The [Michelson interferometer](physical-optics.md#michelson-interferometer) turns a change in an optical path into a shift of [interference fringes](physical-optics.md#interference-fringe). ### Gabriel Lippmann

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)

A [physicist](physicist.md).[Independent biographical identity and professional specialization](physics-bibliography.md#a-propos-de-gabriel-lippmann-by-centre-de-recherche-public-gabriel-lippmann)

[Lippmann photography](optics.md#lippmann-plate) records colour through the depth structure of an exposed emulsion. Its account connects a [standing wave](mathematical-physics.md#standing-wave) to a reflected [optical spectrum](optics.md#optical-spectrum).

### Wilhelm Wien

A [physicist](physicist.md).[Independent biographical identity and professional specialization](https://www.uni-wuerzburg.de/uniarchiv/persoenlichkeiten/gelehrtentafeln/wilhelm-wien/)

[Wien's displacement law](astrophysics.md#wien-s-displacement-law) connects a thermal spectrum's peak position to [temperature](temperature.md). Specifying whether the spectrum is expressed per unit [wavelength](wave-equation.md#wavelength) or per unit [frequency](wave.md#frequency) is essential to stating which peak is meant.

#### Work by Wilhelm Wien

The [Van der Waals equation](pressure.md#van-der-waals-equation) connects a dilute [gas](thermodynamics.md#gas) model to the possibility of a [liquid-gas critical point](critical-phenomenon.md#liquid-gas-critical-point). Its usefulness includes showing how attractions and [excluded volume](statistical-physics.md#excluded-volume) can change an [equation of state](pressure.md#equation-of-state); its simple constants do not make it an exact model for every fluid.

### Albert Einstein

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Einstein)

A [physicist](physicist.md) whose work connects [special relativity](special-relativity.md), [general relativity](general-relativity.md), [Brownian motion](brownian-motion.md) and the [photoelectric effect](quantum-mechanics.md#photoelectric-effect). He worked at the Institute for Advanced Study from 1933 until his death in 1955.[Institutional profile: physicist, life dates and Institute appointment](physics-bibliography.md#albert-einstein-institutional-profile-by-institute-for-advanced-study)

In 1905, [Albert Einstein](#albert-einstein) used the [photoelectric effect](quantum-mechanics.md#photoelectric-effect) to explore quantized [light](optics.md#light), related [Brownian motion](brownian-motion.md) to molecular agitation, formulated [special relativity](special-relativity.md), and connected changes in [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass) and [energy](energy.md). These problems link visible measurements to otherwise inaccessible structure: emitted [electron](physics.md#electron) energies, suspended-particle displacements and comparisons of clocks.[March, May, June and September 1905: the four papers and their physical questions](physics-bibliography.md#albert-einstein-the-great-works-by-center-for-history-of-physics)

The [Millikan photoelectric experiment](quantum-mechanics.md#millikan-photoelectric-experiment) follows one of those predictions into the laboratory. Its stopping-voltage measurements test a quantitative relation; the experiment's apparatus, surface preparation and electrical corrections matter to that inference. The [Einstein field equations](einstein-field-equations.md) and [general relativity](general-relativity.md) lead to a different set of tests involving [gravity](physics.md#gravity) and [spacetime](special-relativity.md#spacetime).

The [Nobel Prize in Physics in 1921](nobel-prize-in-physics.md#nobel-prize-in-physics-in-1921) recognized his theoretical work, particularly the law of the [photoelectric effect](quantum-mechanics.md#photoelectric-effect); the award was presented in 1922.[Prize motivation and the note that Einstein received the 1921 award in 1922](physics-bibliography.md#albert-einstein-facts-by-nobel-prize-outreach)

<a id="image-albert-einstein-in-1947-library-of-congress-portrait-via-wikimedia-commons"></a>
<img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/d3/Albert_Einstein_Head.jpg" alt="" width="240">

**[Figure 80](#image-albert-einstein-in-1947-library-of-congress-portrait-via-wikimedia-commons). Albert Einstein in 1947, Library of Congress portrait via Wikimedia Commons**. [Source](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Albert_Einstein_Head.jpg).

#### Work by Albert Einstein

↑ **Parent:** [Albert Einstein](#albert-einstein)

<h3 id="erwin-schrodinger">Erwin Schrödinger</h3>

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erwin_Schrödinger)

A [physicist](physicist.md) who developed [wave mechanics](quantum-mechanics.md#wave-mechanics). In 1926, [Erwin Schrödinger](#erwin-schrodinger) made the energies of [chemical atoms](physics.md#atom-physics) an [eigenvalue](linear-operator-theory.md#eigenvalue) problem, rather than prescribing a separate quantization rule for each classical [orbit](dynamical-systems.md#orbit-dynamical-system). He shared the [Nobel Prize in Physics in 1933](nobel-prize-in-physics.md#nobel-prize-in-physics-in-1933) with [Paul Dirac](#paul-dirac).[Nobel Lectures, Physics 1922–1941; Zurich period and 1926 wave-equation work; joint 1933 award; birth and death dates](physics-bibliography.md#nobel-lectures-physics-1922-1941/erwin-schrodinger-biographical)

The [Schrödinger equation](mathematical-physics.md#schrodinger-equation) turns a proposed [Hamiltonian operator](quantum-system.md#hamiltonian-quantum-mechanics) into predictions for a [wavefunction](wave-function.md) and its evolution. [Boundary conditions](differential-equation.md#boundary-condition) can select discrete [energy eigenvalues](energy-eigenvalue.md), while [quantum superposition](quantum-system.md#quantum-superposition) makes their relative [quantum phases](quantum-system.md#quantum-phase) observable through [interference](physical-optics.md#interference-wave-propagation). The [infinite square well](quantum-mechanics.md#infinite-square-well) gives a transparent worked example; a [hydrogen atom](mathematical-physics.md#hydrogen-atom) supplies a physical application with a different [potential energy](energy.md#potential-energy).

The [Davisson–Germer experiment](quantum-scattering.md#davisson-germer-experiment) gives a complementary experimental route into [matter waves](wave-function.md#matter-wave): an accelerated [electron](physics.md#electron) beam scattered from a [nickel](chemical-substance.md#nickel) crystal produces direction-dependent collected [electric current](electric-charge.md#electric-current). Its apparatus and angular scans make the wave prediction testable. Interpreting a [wavefunction](wave-function.md) as a [probability amplitude](wave-function.md#probability-amplitude) also requires the [Born rule](wave-function.md#born-rule); that statistical interpretation deserves its own history alongside [Erwin Schrödinger](#erwin-schrodinger)'s dynamics.

<a id="image-erwin-schrodinger-in-1933-nobel-foundation-portrait-via-wikimedia-commons"></a>
<img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/2e/Erwin_Schr%C3%B6dinger_(1933).jpg" alt="" width="240">

**[Figure 81](#image-erwin-schrodinger-in-1933-nobel-foundation-portrait-via-wikimedia-commons). Erwin Schrödinger in 1933, Nobel Foundation portrait via Wikimedia Commons**. [Source](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Erwin_Schrödinger_(1933).jpg).

### John William Strutt, 3rd Baron Rayleigh

↑ **Parent:** [List of physicists](#list-of-physicists)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_William_Strutt,_3rd_Baron_Rayleigh)

A [physicist](physicist.md) whose research joined [waves](wave.md), [optics](optics.md), [acoustics](physics.md#acoustics) and precise measurement. He maintained a laboratory at Terling and served as Cavendish professor of experimental [physics](physics.md) from 1879 to 1884. At Cambridge he developed laboratory courses; later he donated his Nobel award proceeds toward a Cavendish laboratory extension.[O'Connor and Robertson, biography: Terling laboratory; Cavendish professorship and laboratory courses; donation of Nobel proceeds](physics-bibliography.md#john-william-strutt-lord-rayleigh-by-john-joseph-o-connor-and-edmund-frederick-robertson)

The [Rayleigh-Ramsay argon density experiment](chemical-substance.md#rayleigh-ramsay-argon-density-experiment), pursued with [William Ramsay](chemistry.md#william-ramsay), connects a small discrepancy in gas weighing to chemical separation. Its lesson is practical: agreement within one preparation method does not ensure that a second method produces the same [chemical substance](chemical-substance.md). The [gas density](fluid-mechanics.md#gas-density) model explains how composition and filling conditions both affect the readout.

<a id="image-lord-rayleigh-portrait-from-the-mactutor-picture-collection"></a>
<img src="https://mathshistory.st-andrews.ac.uk/Biographies/Rayleigh/Rayleigh_3.jpeg" alt="" width="240">

**[Figure 108](#image-lord-rayleigh-portrait-from-the-mactutor-picture-collection). Lord Rayleigh, portrait from the MacTutor picture collection**.

#### Work by John William Strutt, 3rd Baron Rayleigh

↑ **Parent:** [John William Strutt, 3rd Baron Rayleigh](#john-william-strutt-3rd-baron-rayleigh)
